## LESSON, in learner order

### s01 · explainer · Choose the boundary: JWT signature, issuer, audience and expiry verification
(mechanism) Unverified decoding makes JWT claims readable, not trustworthy. ⟦A.f1 A:tb1 A:tb2 A:tb3⟧
(mechanism) Verify with jwt.decode(token, key, algorithms=[allowed_algorithm], issuer=expected_issuer, audience=expected_audience, options={"require": ["exp", "iss", "aud"]}). Choose the algorithm from trusted configuration, not the token alone. ⟦A.f2 A:tb1 A:tb2 A:tb3⟧
(mechanism) Expiration and not-before constrain time validity. Requiring a claim to exist and checking its value are separate responsibilities. ⟦A.f3 A:tb1 A:tb2 A:tb3⟧
(mechanism) A library verification exception means reject the credential and protected request. Never fall back to using unverified claims. ⟦A.f4 A:tb1 A:tb2 A:tb3⟧
[CODE python]
import jwt
from fastapi import Depends, HTTPException

# key, allowed_algorithm, expected_issuer and expected_audience are trusted configuration.
def verify(token=Depends(existing_bearer_dependency)):
    try:
        return jwt.decode(
            token, key, algorithms=[allowed_algorithm],
            issuer=expected_issuer, audience=expected_audience,
            options={"require": ["exp", "iss", "aud"]},
        )
    except jwt.exceptions.InvalidTokenError:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid credential",
                            headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"})

@app.get("/protected")
def protected(context=Depends(verify)):
    return protected_output(context)
  annotations: ['Require claim presence and verify configured values. Expiration and not-before constrain time validity.', 'Verification failure rejects the request; protected output cannot use unverified fallback claims.']

### s02 · explainer · Trace the distinction: JWT signature, issuer, audience and expiry verification
(mental_model) Keep readable claims, required presence and verified values distinct. Reading a payload establishes only the first. Trusted key, algorithm, issuer and audience configuration set the verification boundary; time checks do not substitute for claim-presence requirements. ⟦A.f1 A.f2 A.f3 A.f4 A:tb4⟧

### s03 · explainer · Trace the distinction: JWT signature, issuer, audience and expiry verification
(walkthrough) Trace valid, expired, future not-before, issuer-mismatch and audience-mismatch fixtures through the verifier. Protected output follows successful verification only. A library rejection must not trigger fallback to the readable payload. The token must not select its own allowed algorithm. ⟦A.f1 A.f2 A.f3 A.f4 A:tb5 A:tb6⟧

### s04 · check · Distinguish: JWT signature, issuer, audience and expiry verification
Check your understanding — 
QUESTION [choice, intro, grading=client] A verified-signature token is expired, or its not-before time is still in the future. What should happen?
  ✔ o1: Reject the credential for failing its time-validity constraint.
  ✘ o2: Accept it because signature verification cancels time checks.
      why: Signature and time validity are distinct constraints.
  ✘ o3: Use decoded claims without verification as a fallback.
      why: Readable claims do not establish trust and verification failures must be rejected.
  feedback✔: You preserved the intended boundary. Expiration and not-before claims constrain token time validity; checks for a claim's value are distinct from requiring that the claim be present. Treat a library verification exception as an invalid credential and reject the protected request rather than using its unverified claims.  feedback✘: Review the relevant boundary and compare each required outcome. ### s05 · check · Distinguish: JWT signature, issuer, audience and expiry verification
Check your understanding — 
QUESTION [choice, core, grading=client] Why configure options={"require": ["exp", "iss", "aud"]} as well as the trusted issuer and audience?
  ✔ o1: Required presence and valid claim values are distinct checks.
  ✘ o2: Matching issuer/audience configuration makes every required claim automatically present.
      why: Claim-value expectations are not the same as requiring claim presence.
  ✘ o3: Claim presence alone proves the signature and every value valid.
      why: Presence is only one constraint; trusted verification must still check signature and configured values.

EllipticCurvePublicKey | Ed25519PublicKey | Ed448PublicKey\\n    )\\n\\n\\nrequires_cryptography = {\\n    "RS256",\\n    "RS384",\\n    "RS512",\\n    "ES256",\\n    "ES256K",\\n    "ES384",\\n    "ES521",\\n    "ES512",\\n    "PS256",\\n    "PS384",\\n    "PS512",\\n    "EdDSA",\\n}\\n\\n\\ndef get_default_algorithms() -> dict[str, Algorithm]:\\n    """\\n    Returns the algorithms that are implemented by the library.\\n    """\\n    default_algorithms = {\\n        "none": NoneAlgorithm(),\\n        "HS256": HMACAlgorithm(HMACAlgorithm.SHA256),\\n        "HS384": HMACAlgorithm(HMACAlgorithm.SHA384),\\n        "HS512": HMACAlgorithm(HMACAlgorithm.SHA512),\\n    }\\n\\n    if has_crypto:\\n        default_algorithms.update(\\n            {\\n                "RS256": RSAAlgorithm(RSAAlgorithm.SHA256),\\n                "RS384": RSAAlgorithm(RSAAlgorithm.SHA384),\\n                "RS512": RSAAlgorithm(RSAAlgorithm.SHA512),\\n                "ES256": ECAlgorithm(ECAlgorithm.SHA256),\\n                "ES256K": ECAlgorithm(ECAlgorithm.SHA256),\\n                "ES384": ECAlgorithm(ECAlgorithm.SHA384),\\n                "ES521": ECAlgorithm(ECAlgorithm.SHA512),\\n                "ES512": ECAlgorithm(\\n                    ECAlgorithm.SHA512\\n                ),  # Backward compat for #219 fix\\n                "PS256": RSAPSSAlgorithm(RSAPSSAlgorithm.SHA256),\\n                "PS384": RSAPSSAlgorithm(RSAPSSAlgorithm.SHA384),\\n                "PS512": RSAPSSAlgorithm(RSAPSSAlgorithm.SHA512),\\n                "EdDSA": OKPAlgorithm(),\\n            }\\n        )\\n\\n    return default_algorithms\\n\\n\\nclass Algorithm(ABC):\\n    """\\n    The interface for an algorithm used to sign and verify tokens.\\n    """\\n\\n    def compute_hash_digest(self, bytestr: bytes) -> bytes:\\n        """\\n        Compute a hash digest using the specified algorithm\\\'s hash algorithm.\\n\\n        If there is no hash algorithm, raises a NotImplementedError.\\n        """\\n        # lookup self.hash_alg if defined in a way that mypy can understand\\n        hash_alg = getattr(self, "hash_alg", None)\\n        if hash_alg is None:\\n            raise NotImplementedError\\n\\n        if (\\n            has_crypto\\n            and isinstance(hash_alg, type)\\n            and issubclass(hash_alg, hashes.HashAlgorithm)\\n        ):\\n            digest = hashes.Hash(hash_alg(), backend=default_backend())\\n            digest.update(bytestr)\\n            return bytes(digest.finalize())\\n        else:\\n            return bytes(hash_alg(bytestr).digest())\\n\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def prepare_key(self, key: Any) -> Any:\\n        """\\n        Performs necessary validation and conversions on the key and returns\\n        the key value in the proper format for sign() and verify().\\n        """\\n\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def sign(self, msg: bytes, key: Any) -> bytes:\\n        """\\n        Returns a digital signature for the specified message\\n        using the specified key value.\\n        """\\n\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def verify(self, msg: bytes, key: Any, sig: bytes) -> bool:\\n        """\\n        Verifies that the specified digital signature is valid\\n        for the specified message and key values.\\n        """\\n\\n    @overload\\n    @staticmethod\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def to_jwk(key_obj, as_dict: Literal[True]) -> JWKDict: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n    @overload\\n    @staticmethod\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def to_jwk(key_obj, as_dict: Literal[False] = False) -> str: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def to_jwk(key_obj, as_dict: bool = False) -> JWKDict | str:\\n        """\\n        Serializes a given key into a JWK\\n        """\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def from_jwk(jwk: str | JWKDict) -> Any:\\n        """\\n        Deserializes a given key from JWK back into a key object\\n        """\\n\\n\\nclass NoneAlgorithm(Algorithm):\\n    """\\n    Placeholder for use when no signing or verification\\n    operations are required.\\n    """\\n\\n    def prepare_key(self, key: str | None) -> None:\\n        if key == "":\\n            key = None\\n\\n        if key is not None:\\n            raise InvalidKeyError(\\\'When alg = "none", key value must be None.\\\')\\n\\n        return key\\n\\n    def sign(self, msg: bytes, key: None) -> bytes:\\n        return b""\\n\\n    def verify(self, msg: bytes, key: None, sig: bytes) -> bool:\\n        return False\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def to_jwk(key_obj: Any, as_dict: bool = False) -> NoReturn:\\n        raise NotImplementedError()\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def from_jwk(jwk: str | JWKDict) -> NoReturn:\\n        raise NotImplementedError()\\n\\n\\nclass HMACAlgorithm(Algorithm):\\n    """\\n    Performs signing and verification operations using HMAC\\n    and the specified hash function.\\n    """\\n\\n    SHA256: ClassVar[HashlibHash] = hashlib.sha256\\n    SHA384: ClassVar[HashlibHash] = hashlib.sha384\\n    SHA512: ClassVar[HashlibHash] = hashlib.sha512\\n\\n    def __init__(self, hash_alg: HashlibHash) -> None:\\n        self.hash_alg = hash_alg\\n\\n    def prepare_key(self, key: str | bytes) -> bytes:\\n        key_bytes = force_bytes(key)\\n\\n        if is_pem_format(key_bytes) or is_ssh_key(key_bytes):\\n            raise InvalidKeyError(\\n                "The specified key is an asymmetric key or x509 certificate and"\\n                " should not be used as an HMAC secret."\\n            )\\n\\n        return key_bytes\\n\\n    @overload\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def to_jwk(\\n        key_obj: str | bytes, as_dict: Literal[True]\\n    ) -> JWKDict: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n    @overload\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def to_jwk(\\n        key_obj: str | bytes, as_dict: Literal[False] = False\\n    ) -> str: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def to_jwk(key_obj: str | bytes, as_dict: bool = False) -> JWKDict | str:\\n        jwk = {\\n            "k": base64url_encode(force_bytes(key_obj)).decode(),\\n            "kty": "oct",\\n        }\\n\\n        if as_dict:\\n            return jwk\\n        else:\\n            return json.dumps(jwk)\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def from_jwk(jwk: str | JWKDict) -> bytes:\\n        try:\\n            if isinstance(jwk, str):\\n                obj: JWKDict = json.loads(jwk)\\n            elif isinstance(jwk, dict):\\n                obj = jwk\\n            else:\\n                raise ValueError\\n        except ValueError:\\n            raise InvalidKeyError("Key is not valid JSON") from None\\n\\n        if obj.get("kty") != "oct":\\n            raise InvalidKeyError("Not an HMAC key")\\n\\n        return base64url_decode(obj["k"])\\n\\n    def sign(self, msg: bytes, key: bytes) -> bytes:\\n        return hmac.new(key, msg, self.hash_alg).digest()\\n\\n    def verify(self, msg: bytes, key: bytes, sig: bytes) -> bool:\\n        return hmac.compare_digest(sig, self.sign(msg, key))\\n\\n\\nif has_crypto:\\n\\n    class RSAAlgorithm(Algorithm):\\n        """\\n        Performs signing and verification operations using\\n        RSASSA-PKCS-v1_5 and the specified hash function.\\n        """\\n\\n        SHA256: ClassVar[type[hashes.HashAlgorithm]] = hashes.SHA256\\n        SHA384: ClassVar[type[hashes.HashAlgorithm]] = hashes.SHA384\\n        SHA512: ClassVar[type[hashes.HashAlgorithm]] = hashes.SHA512\\n\\n        def __init__(self, hash_alg: type[hashes.HashAlgorithm]) -> None:\\n            self.hash_alg = hash_alg\\n\\n        def prepare_key(self, key: AllowedRSAKeys | str | bytes) -> AllowedRSAKeys:\\n            if isinstance(key, (RSAPrivateKey, RSAPublicKey)):\\n                return key\\n\\n            if not isinstance(key, (bytes, str)):\\n                raise TypeError("Expecting a PEM-formatted key.")\\n\\n            key_bytes = force_bytes(key)\\n\\n            try:\\n                if key_bytes.startswith(b"ssh-rsa"):\\n                    return cast(RSAPublicKey, load_ssh_public_key(key_bytes))\\n                else:\\n                    return cast(\\n                        RSAPrivateKey, load_pem_private_key(key_bytes, password=None)\\n                    )\\n            except ValueError:\\n                try:\\n                    return cast(RSAPublicKey, load_pem_public_key(key_bytes))\\n                except (ValueError, UnsupportedAlgorithm):\\n                    raise InvalidKeyError(\\n                        "Could not parse the provided public key."\\n                    ) from None\\n\\n        @overload\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def to_jwk(\\n            key_obj: AllowedRSAKeys, as_dict: Literal[True]\\n        ) -> JWKDict: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n        @overload\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def to_jwk(\\n            key_obj: AllowedRSAKeys, as_dict: Literal[False] = False\\n        ) -> str: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def to_jwk(key_obj: AllowedRSAKeys, as_dict: bool = False) -> JWKDict | str:\\n            obj: dict[str, Any] | None = None\\n\\n            if hasattr(key_obj, "private_numbers"):\\n                # Private key\\n                numbers = key_obj.private_numbers()\\n\\n                obj = {\\n                    "kty": "RSA",\\n                    "key_ops": ["sign"],\\n                    "n": to_base64url_uint(numbers.public_numbers.n).decode(),\\n                    "e": to_base64url_uint(numbers.public_numbers.e).decode(),\\n                    "d": to_base64url_uint(numbers.d).decode(),\\n                    "p": to_base64url_uint(numbers.p).decode(),\\n                    "q": to_base64url_uint(numbers.q).decode(),\\n                    "dp": to_base64url_uint(numbers.dmp1).decode(),\\n                    "dq": to_base64url_uint(numbers.dmq1).decode(),\\n                    "qi": to_base64url_uint(numbers.iqmp).decode(),\\n                }\\n\\n            elif hasattr(key_obj, "verify"):\\n                # Public key\\n                numbers = key_obj.public_numbers()\\n\\n                obj = {\\n                    "kty": "RSA",\\n                    "key_ops": ["verify"],\\n                    "n": to_base64url_uint(numbers.n).decode(),\\n                    "e": to_base64url_uint(numbers.e).decode(),\\n                }\\n            else:\\n                raise InvalidKeyError("Not a public or private key")\\n\\n            if as_dict:\\n                return obj\\n            else:\\n                return json.dumps(obj)\\n\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def from_jwk(jwk: str | JWKDict) -> AllowedRSAKeys:\\n            try:\\n                if isinstance(jwk, str):\\n                    obj = json.loads(jwk)\\n                elif isinstance(jwk, dict):\\n                    obj = jwk\\n                else:\\n                    raise ValueError\\n            except ValueError:\\n                raise InvalidKeyError("Key is not valid JSON") from None\\n\\n            if obj.get("kty") != "RSA":\\n                raise InvalidKeyError("Not an RSA key") from None\\n\\n            if "d" in obj and "e" in obj and "n" in obj:\\n                # Private key\\n                if "oth" in obj:\\n                    raise InvalidKeyError(\\n                        "Unsupported RSA private key: > 2 primes not supported"\\n                    )\\n\\n                other_props = ["p", "q", "dp", "dq", "qi"]\\n                props_found = [prop in obj for prop in other_props]\\n                any_props_found = any(props_found)\\n\\n                if any_props_found and not all(props_found):\\n                    raise InvalidKeyError(\\n                        "RSA key must include all parameters if any are present besides d"\\n                    ) from None\\n\\n                public_numbers = RSAPublicNumbers(\\n                    from_base64url_uint(obj["e"]),\\n                    from_base64url_uint(obj["n"]),\\n                )\\n\\n                if any_props_found:\\n                    numbers = RSAPrivateNumbers(\\n                        d=from_base64url_uint(obj["d"]),\\n                        p=from_base64url_uint(obj["p"]),\\n                        q=from_base64url_uint(obj["q"]),\\n                        dmp1=from_base64url_uint(obj["dp"]),\\n                        dmq1=from_base64url_uint(obj["dq"]),\\n                        iqmp=from_base64url_uint(obj["qi"]),\\n                        public_numbers=public_numbers,\\n                    )\\n                else:\\n                    d = from_base64url_uint(obj["d"])\\n                    p, q = rsa_recover_prime_factors(\\n                        public_numbers.n, d, public_numbers.e\\n                    )\\n\\n                    numbers = RSAPrivateNumbers(\\n                        d=d,\\n                        p=p,\\n                        q=q,\\n                        dmp1=rsa_crt_dmp1(d, p),\\n                        dmq1=rsa_crt_dmq1(d, q),\\n                        iqmp=rsa_crt_iqmp(p, q),\\n                        public_numbers=public_numbers,\\n                    )\\n\\n                return numbers.private_key()\\n            elif "n" in obj and "e" in obj:\\n                # Public key\\n                return RSAPublicNumbers(\\n                    from_base64url_uint(obj["e"]),\\n                    from_base64url_uint(obj["n"]),\\n                ).public_key()\\n            else:\\n                raise InvalidKeyError("Not a public or private key")\\n\\n        def sign(self, msg: bytes, key: RSAPrivateKey) -> bytes:\\n            return key.sign(msg, padding.PKCS1v15(), self.hash_alg())\\n\\n        def verify(self, msg: bytes, key: RSAPublicKey, sig: bytes) -> bool:\\n            try:\\n                key.verify(sig, msg, padding.PKCS1v15(), self.hash_alg())\\n                return True\\n            except InvalidSignature:\\n                return False\\n\\n    class ECAlgorithm(Algorithm):\\n        """\\n        Performs signing and verification operations using\\n        ECDSA and the specified hash function\\n        """\\n\\n        SHA256: ClassVar[type[hashes.HashAlgorithm]] = hashes.SHA256\\n        SHA384: ClassVar[type[hashes.HashAlgorithm]] = hashes.SHA384\\n        SHA512: ClassVar[type[hashes.HashAlgorithm]] = hashes.SHA512\\n\\n        def __init__(self, hash_alg: type[hashes.HashAlgorithm]) -> None:\\n            self.hash_alg = hash_alg\\n\\n        def prepare_key(self, key: AllowedECKeys | str | bytes) -> AllowedECKeys:\\n            if isinstance(key, (EllipticCurvePrivateKey, EllipticCurvePublicKey)):\\n                return key\\n\\n            if not isinstance(key, (bytes, str)):\\n                raise TypeError("Expecting a PEM-formatted key.")\\n\\n            key_bytes = force_bytes(key)\\n\\n            # Attempt to load key. We don\\\'t know if it\\\'s\\n            # a Signing Key or a Verifying Key, so we try\\n            # the Verifying Key first.\\n            try:\\n                if key_bytes.startswith(b"ecdsa-sha2-"):\\n                    crypto_key = load_ssh_public_key(key_bytes)\\n                else:\\n                    crypto_key = load_pem_public_key(key_bytes)  # type: ignore[assignment]\\n            except ValueError:\\n                crypto_key = load_pem_private_key(key_bytes, password=None)  # type: ignore[assignment]\\n\\n            # Explicit check the key to prevent confusing errors from cryptography\\n            if not isinstance(\\n                crypto_key, (EllipticCurvePrivateKey, EllipticCurvePublicKey)\\n            ):\\n                raise InvalidKeyError(\\n                    "Expecting a EllipticCurvePrivateKey/EllipticCurvePublicKey. Wrong key provided for ECDSA algorithms"\\n                ) from None\\n\\n            return crypto_key\\n\\n        def sign(self, msg: bytes, key: EllipticCurvePrivateKey) -> bytes:\\n            der_sig = key.sign(msg, ECDSA(self.hash_alg()))\\n\\n            return der_to_raw_signature(der_sig, key.curve)\\n\\n        def verify(self, msg: bytes, key: AllowedECKeys, sig: bytes) -> bool:\\n            try:\\n                der_sig = raw_to_der_signature(sig, key.curve)\\n            except ValueError:\\n                return False\\n\\n            try:\\n                public_key = (\\n                    key.public_key()\\n                    if isinstance(key, EllipticCurvePrivateKey)\\n                    else key\\n                )\\n                public_key.verify(der_sig, msg, ECDSA(self.hash_alg()))\\n                return True\\n            except InvalidSignature:\\n                return False\\n\\n        @overload\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def to_jwk(\\n            key_obj: AllowedECKeys, as_dict: Literal[True]\\n        ) -> JWKDict: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n        @overload\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def to_jwk(\\n            key_obj: AllowedECKeys, as_dict: Literal[False] = False\\n        ) -> str: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def to_jwk(key_obj: AllowedECKeys, as_dict: bool = False) -> JWKDict | str:\\n            if isinstance(key_obj, EllipticCurvePrivateKey):\\n                public_numbers = key_obj.public_key().public_numbers()\\n            elif isinstance(key_obj, EllipticCurvePublicKey):\\n                public_numbers = key_obj.public_numbers()\\n            else:\\n                raise InvalidKeyError("Not a public or private key")\\n\\n            if isinstance(key_obj.curve, SECP256R1):\\n                crv = "P-256"\\n            elif isinstance(key_obj.curve, SECP384R1):\\n                crv = "P-384"\\n            elif isinstance(key_obj.curve, SECP521R1):\\n                crv = "P-521"\\n            elif isinstance(key_obj.curve, SECP256K1):\\n                crv = "secp256k1"\\n            else:\\n                raise InvalidKeyError(f"Invalid curve: {key_obj.curve}")\\n\\n            obj: dict[str, Any] = {\\n                "kty": "EC",\\n                "crv": crv,\\n                "x": to_base64url_uint(\\n                    public_numbers.x,\\n                    bit_length=key_obj.curve.key_size,\\n                ).decode(),\\n                "y": to_base64url_uint(\\n                    public_numbers.y,\\n                    bit_length=key_obj.curve.key_size,\\n                ).decode(),\\n            }\\n\\n            if isinstance(key_obj, EllipticCurvePrivateKey):\\n                obj["d"] = to_base64url_uint(\\n                    key_obj.private_numbers().private_value,\\n                    bit_length=key_obj.curve.key_size,\\n                ).decode()\\n\\n            if as_dict:\\n                return obj\\n            else:\\n                return json.dumps(obj)\\n\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def from_jwk(jwk: str | JWKDict) -> AllowedECKeys:\\n            try:\\n                if isinstance(jwk, str):\\n                    obj = json.loads(jwk)\\n                elif isinstance(jwk, dict):\\n                    obj = jwk\\n                else:\\n                    raise ValueError\\n            except ValueError:\\n                raise InvalidKeyError("Key is not valid JSON") from None\\n\\n            if obj.get("kty") != "EC":\\n from None\\n\\n            x = base64url_decode(obj.get("x"))\\n            y = base64url_decode(obj.get("y"))\\n\\n            curve = obj.get("crv")\\n            curve_obj: EllipticCurve\\n\\n curve_obj = SECP384R1()\\n                else:\\n                    raise InvalidKeyError(\\n                        "Coords should be 48 bytes for curve P-384"\\n                    ) from None\\n            elif curve == "P-521":\\n                if len(x) == len(y) == 66:\\n                    curve_obj = SECP521R1()\\n                else:\\n                    raise InvalidKeyError(\\n        else:\\n                raise InvalidKeyError(f"Invalid curve: {curve}")\\n\\n            public_numbers = EllipticCurvePublicNumbers(\\n                x=int.from_bytes(x, byteorder="big"),\\n                y=int.from_bytes(y, byteorder="big"),\\n                curve=curve_obj,\\n            )\\n\\n            if "d" not in obj:\\n                return public_numbers.public_key()\\n\\n            d = base64url_decode(obj.get("d"))\\n            if len(d) != len(x):\\n                raise InvalidKeyError(\\n                    "D should be {} bytes for curve {}", len(x), curve\\n                )\\n\\n            return EllipticCurvePrivateNumbers(\\n                int.from_bytes(d, byteorder="big"), public_numbers\\n            ).private_key()\\n\\n    class RSAPSSAlgorithm(RSAAlgorithm):\\n        """\\n        Performs a signature using RSASSA-PSS with MGF1\\n        """\\n\\n        def sign(self, msg: bytes, key: RSAPrivateKey) -> bytes:\\n            return key.sign(\\n                msg,\\n                padding.PSS(\\n                    mgf=padding.MGF1(self.hash_alg()),\\n                    salt_length=self.hash_alg().digest_size,\\n                ),\\n                self.hash_alg(),\\n            )\\n\\n        def verify(self, msg: bytes, key: RSAPublicKey, sig: bytes) -> bool:\\n            try:\\n                key.verify(\\n                    sig,\\n                    msg,\\n                    padding.PSS(\\n                        mgf=padding.MGF1(self.hash_alg()),\\n                        salt_length=self.hash_alg().digest_size,\\n                    ),\\n                    self.hash_alg(),\\n                )\\n                return True\\n            except InvalidSignature:\\n                return False\\n\\n    class OKPAlgorithm(Algorithm):\\n        """\\n        Performs signing and verification operations using EdDSA\\n\\n        This class requires ``cryptography>=2.6`` to be installed.\\n        """\\n\\n        def __init__(self, **kwargs: Any) -> None:\\n            pass\\n\\n        def prepare_key(self, key: AllowedOKPKeys | str | bytes) -> AllowedOKPKeys:\\n            if isinstance(key, (bytes, str)):\\n                key_str = key.decode("utf-8") if isinstance(key, bytes) else key\\n                key_bytes = key.encode("utf-8") if isinstance(key, str) else key\\n\\n                if "-----BEGIN PUBLIC" in key_str:\\n                    key = load_pem_public_key(key_bytes)  # type: ignore[assignment]\\n                elif "-----BEGIN PRIVATE" in key_str:\\n                    key = load_pem_private_key(key_bytes, password=None)  # type: ignore[assignment]\\n                elif key_str[0:4] == "ssh-":\\n                    key = load_ssh_public_key(key_bytes)  # type: ignore[assignment]\\n\\n            # Explicit check the key to prevent confusing errors from cryptography\\n            if not isinstance(\\n                key,\\n                (Ed25519PrivateKey, Ed25519PublicKey, Ed448PrivateKey, Ed448PublicKey),\\n            ):\\n                raise InvalidKeyError(\\n                    "Expecting a EllipticCurvePrivateKey/EllipticCurvePublicKey. Wrong key provided for EdDSA algorithms"\\n                )\\n\\n            return key\\n\\n        def sign(\\n            self, msg: str | bytes, key: Ed25519PrivateKey | Ed448PrivateKey\\n        ) -> bytes:\\n            """\\n            Sign a message ``msg`` using the EdDSA private key ``key``\\n            :param str|bytes msg: Message to sign\\n            :param Ed25519PrivateKey}Ed448PrivateKey key: A :class:`.Ed25519PrivateKey`\\n                or :class:`.Ed448PrivateKey` isinstance\\n            :return bytes signature: The signature, as bytes\\n            """\\n            msg_bytes = msg.encode("utf-8") if isinstance(msg, str) else msg\\n            return key.sign(msg_bytes)\\n\\n        def verify(\\n            self, msg: str | bytes, key: AllowedOKPKeys, sig: str | bytes\\n        ) -> bool:\\n            """\\n            Verify a given ``msg`` against a signature ``sig`` using the EdDSA key ``key``\\n\\n            :param str|bytes sig: EdDSA signature to check ``msg`` against\\n            :param str|bytes msg: Message to sign\\n            :param Ed25519PrivateKey|Ed25519PublicKey|Ed448PrivateKey|Ed448PublicKey key:\\n                A private or public EdDSA key instance\\n            :return bool verified: True if signature is valid, False if not.\\n            """\\n            try:\\n                msg_bytes = msg.encode("utf-8") if isinstance(msg, str) else msg\\n                sig_bytes = sig.encode("utf-8") if isinstance(sig, str) else sig\\n\\n                public_key = (\\n                    key.public_key()\\n                    if isinstance(key, (Ed25519PrivateKey, Ed448PrivateKey))\\n                    else key\\n                )\\n                public_key.verify(sig_bytes, msg_bytes)\\n                return True  # If no exception was raised, the signature is valid.\\n            except InvalidSignature:\\n                return False\\n\\n        @overload\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def to_jwk(\\n            key: AllowedOKPKeys, as_dict: Literal[True]\\n        ) -> JWKDict: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n        @overload\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def to_jwk(\\n            key: AllowedOKPKeys, as_dict: Literal[False] = False\\n        ) -> str: ...  # pragma: no cover\\n\\n        @staticmethod\\n        def to_jwk(key: AllowedOKPKeys, as_dict: bool = False) -> JWKDict | str:\\n            if isinstance(key, (Ed25519PublicKey, Ed448PublicKey)):\\n                x = key.public_bytes(\\n                    encoding=Encoding.Raw,\\n                    format=PublicFormat.Raw,\\n                )\\n                crv = "Ed25519" if isinstance(key, Ed25519PublicKey) else "Ed448"\\n\\n                obj = {\\n                    "x": base64url_encode(force_bytes(x)).decode(),\\n                    "kty": "OKP",\\n                    "crv": crv,\\n                }\\n\\n                if as_dict:\\n                    return obj\\n                else:\\n                    return json.dumps(obj)\\n\\n            if isinstance(key, (Ed25519PrivateKey, Ed448PrivateKey)):\\n                d = key.private_bytes(\\n async def verify(token: Annotated[str, Depends(extract)]):
    # TODO: trusted verification arguments, required claims, rejection on library errors.
    try:
        return jwt.decode(token, key, algorithms=[allowed_algorithm], issuer=expected_issuer, audience=expected_audience, options={"require": ["exp", "iss", "aud"]})
    except jwt.exceptions.InvalidTokenError:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid credential", headers={"WWW-Authenticate": "Bearer"})

@app.get("/protected")
async def protected(claims: Annotated[dict, Depends(verify)]):
    # TODO: attach verification dependency; protected output only after acceptance.
    return {"protected":True}

## SOURCE FACTS — A (cache.freshness_policy)   objective: Control cached-data freshness after writes: implement expiry and write invalidation for one cached resource.
A.f1: Choose a cache TTL from the tolerated staleness window: a shorter TTL refreshes sooner but causes more primary-store reads, while a longer TTL reduces reads and can serve older values longer.   [sources: redis_docs#https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/use-cases/cache-aside/redis-py/#choose-a-ttl-that-matches-your-staleness-tolerance]
A.f2: For a string cache entry, pass ex=seconds to Redis set(key, value, ex=seconds) to assign an expiry when writing it.   [sources: redis_docs#https://redis.readthedocs.io/en/stable/commands.html#redis.commands.core.CoreCommands.set]
A.f3: After the primary record changes, delete its cache key so the next cache-aside read reloads it instead of serving the old copy.   [sources: redis_docs#https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/use-cases/cache-aside/redis-py/#invalidate-dont-try-to-keep-the-cache-in-sync]
A.f4: Inspect ttl(key): it reports remaining seconds, -1 for a key without expiry and -2 for an absent key; change the primary record and verify that invalidation makes the next read return the changed value.   [sources: redis_docs#https://redis.io/commands/ttl, redis_docs#https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/use-cases/cache-aside/redis-py/#inspect-cached-entries-directly-in-redis]
note: Establish this first: Freshness is bounded by lifetime and invalidation of derived copies.
note: Stay within the current objective: Control cached-data freshness after writes. Avoid extending the example into blocked later concepts.
note: Use the worked case to support this target: Select expiry and invalidation satisfying a stated stale-data tolerance.
PRACTICE ANCHOR (live_code): Implement expiry and write invalidation for one cached order. Choose a positive TTL within a stated stale-data tolerance, assign it when caching the order's notes, delete the cache key after a committed notes change, and verify the next cache-aside read returns the changed notes.  SUCCESS: The cached notes have a positive remaining TTL no greater than the chosen lifetime. After the primary notes change and the cache key is deleted, ttl(key) returns -2 before the next read. That read returns the new notes and repopulates the cache with expiry.
ASSESSMENT ANCHOR: select_and_configure_expiry_for_staleness_tolerance facts=['f1', 'f2']
ASSESSMENT ANCHOR: select_write_invalidation_after_primary_changes facts=['f3']
ASSESSMENT ANCHOR: interpret_ttl_and_verify_freshness_after_invalidation facts=['f4']

## SOURCE FACTS — B (cache.safe_failure_policy)   objective: Preserve correctness when the cache is unavailable: implement a correct database fallback for one cache-backed read.
B.f1: Catch Redis connection and timeout exceptions at the cache boundary so the application can apply its declared cache-outage policy rather than letting an incidental cache exception choose the outcome.   [sources: redis_docs#https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/clients/redis-py/produsage/#exception-handling]
B.f2: When the cache is only an acceleration layer and the primary store is available, the miss path can load and return the primary-store value without a successful cache read or write.   [sources: redis_docs#https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/use-cases/cache-aside/redis-py/#the-cache-aside-helper, redis_docs#https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/use-cases/cache-aside/#when-to-use-redis-cache-aside]
B.f3: Configure finite socket_connect_timeout and socket_timeout values on the Redis client so a failed cache does not cause unbounded connection or response waits.   [sources: redis_docs#https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/clients/redis-py/produsage/#timeouts]
B.f4: Verify the policy with a controlled failing cache dependency, asserting the primary-store result or the declared failure outcome rather than assuming that a cache outage is equivalent to lost primary data.   [sources: redis_docs#https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/clients/redis-py/produsage/#exception-handling, fastapi_docs_tutorial#https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/tutorial/testing/#using-testclient]
note: Establish this first: A cache outage may degrade performance but must not change the authoritative result..
note: Stay within the current objective: Preserve correctness when the cache is unavailable. Avoid extending the example into blocked later concepts.
note: Use the worked case to support this target: Determine whether cache failure changes authoritative outcomes.
PRACTICE ANCHOR (live_code): Implement one cache-backed read that returns the available primary-store value when Redis connection or timeout failures prevent cache lookup or population. Configure finite Redis connection and response timeouts and verify the fallback using a controlled failing cache dependency.  SUCCESS: The read returns the primary-store value when cache lookup fails and when cache population fails after a miss. Controlled connection and timeout failures do not escape the cache boundary, and both Redis socket timeout settings are finite.
ASSESSMENT ANCHOR: determine_whether_cache_failure_changes_authoritative_outcomes facts=['f1', 'f2', 'f4']
ASSESSMENT ANCHOR: identify_finite_cache_connection_and_response_waits facts=['f3']

### s02 · explainer · Trace the distinction: Freshness policy
(mental_model) The fixture permits 30 seconds of staleness for order:42:notes. Compare old notes old note 42 with changed notes new note 42. After the committed primary change, delete the key and inspect TTL before the next read. No expiry and no key are different states. Verify fresh reload and expiry when the copy is populated again. ⟦A.f1 A.f2 A.f3 A.f4 A:tb4 A:tb5⟧

### s03 · video · Follow the sequence: Freshness policy
[VIDEO Expiry and invalidation keep derived copies fresh] bridge-before: Watch how a changed project list leaves its cached copy stale, and how expiry or deletion causes the next read to reload. The clip calls explicit deletion “active invalidation”; Devspace uses “write invalidation.” | bridge-after: The clip explains deletion after writes and expiry as a safety net. The text adds the shorter-versus-longer TTL read-load tradeoff, Python ex syntax, TTL inspection meanings, and changed-value verification.
  - mRU2PsyOLRk 234-332s facts=['f3'] label: How expiry and write invalidation trigger a fresh read | summary: A cached project list becomes stale when a new project is added to the database without changing the cached copy. Giving the cache key a 60-second lifetime allows that old copy to remain until expiry; the next read then misses and reloads from the database. Write invalidation instead deletes the affected key when the primary data changes, so the next read reloads rather than returning the old list. Every relevant write path needs that deletion. Combining expiry with write invalidation limits how long a missed invalidation can leave an old copy available.

### s04 · check · Distinguish: Freshness policy
Check your understanding — 
QUESTION [choice, intro, grading=client] After a committed notes change, the cache key is deleted and ttl(key) reports -2. What is the expected next cache-aside read?
  ✔ o1: Load the changed primary notes and repopulate with expiry; -2 means the key is absent.
  ✘ o2: Serve the old cached notes because -2 means no expiry.
      why: -1 means an existing key without expiry; -2 means absence.
  ✘ o3: Return the old notes from the cache because deleting the key changes primary truth.
      why: Invalidation removes a cached copy; the next miss loads changed primary data.
  feedback✔: You preserved the intended boundary. _SUPPLIED_SCAFFOLD = 'import asyncio,csv,time,json\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nclass PrimaryStore:\n    def __init__(self):\n        with open(Path("orders.csv"), newline="") as handle:\n            self.rows = {int(r["id"]): r for r in csv.DictReader(handle)}\n        self.calls = []\n    async def load_order(self, order_id, representation):\n        self.calls.append((order_id, representation))\n        r = self.rows[order_id]\n        if representation == "notes":\n            return {"order_id":order_id, "notes":r["note"]}\n        return {"order_id":order_id, "quantity":int(r["quantity"])}\n    async def committed_notes_update(self, order_id, notes):\n        self.rows[order_id]["note"] = notes\n\nclass Cache:\n    # Endpoint-ready adapter for cache-aside; not raw Redis serialization.\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.entries = {}; self.expiry = {}; self.calls = []\n    async def get(self, key):\n        self.calls.append(("get", key))\n        if key in self.expiry and self.expiry[key] <= time.monotonic():\n            self.entries.pop(key, None); self.expiry.pop(key, None)\n        return self.entries.get(key)\n    async def set(self, key, value, ex=None):\n        self.calls.append(("set", key, ex)); self.entries[key] = value\n        if ex is None: self.expiry.pop(key, None)\n        else: self.expiry[key] = time.monotonic()+ex\n    async def delete(self, key):\n        self.calls.append(("delete", key)); self.entries.pop(key, None); self.expiry.pop(key, None)\n    async def ttl(self, key):\n        if await self.get(key) is None: return -2\n        if key not in self.expiry: return -1\n        return max(0, int(self.expiry[key]-time.monotonic()))\n\nclass FailingCache(Cache):\n    def __init__(self, operation, exception):\n        super().__init__(); self.operation=operation; self.exception=exception\n    async def get(self, key):\n        if self.operation == "get": raise self.exception("synthetic cache lookup failure")\n        return None\n    async def set(self, key, value, ex=None):\n        if self.operation == "set": raise self.exception("synthetic cache population failure")\n        return await super().set(key,value,ex)\n\nfreshness_contract={\'order_id\': 42, \'cache_key\': \'order:42:notes\', \'old_notes\': \'old note 42\', \'new_notes\': \'new note 42\', \'tolerance_seconds\': 30, \'ttl_sentinels\': {\'no_expiry\': -1, \'absent_key\': -2}, \'source_concept_id\': \'cache.freshness_policy\', \'objective\': "Implement expiry and write invalidation for one cached order. Choose a positive TTL within a stated stale-data tolerance, assign it when caching the order\'s notes, delete the cache key after a committed notes change, and verify the next cache-aside read returns the changed notes.", \'success\': \'The cached notes have a positive remaining TTL no greater than the chosen lifetime. After the primary notes change and the cache key is deleted, ttl(key) returns -2 before the next read. That read returns the new notes and repopulates the cache with expiry.\', \'live_code\': {\'required_skill\': \'Use Redis string-entry expiry, key deletion after a successful primary write, and TTL inspection within an existing async cache-aside implementation.\', \'allowed_syntax\': [\'async def\', \'await\', \'variable assignment\', \'if\', \'return\', \'assert\', \'string literals\', \'integer literals\', \'comparisons\', \'await redis.get(key)\', \'await redis.set(key, value, ex=seconds)\', \'await redis.delete(key)\', \'await redis.ttl(key)\', \'calls to supplied order retrieval and committed notes-update helpers\'], \'blocked_syntax\': [\'Redis exception handlers that substitute a primary-store read\', \'retry loops around Redis operations\', \'Redis pipeline or transaction commands\', \'Redis Lua scripting\'], \'starter_state_intent\': \'Provide a connected async Redis client, one order cache key, synthetic old and new notes, a stated stale-data tolerance, and supplied helpers for primary-order retrieval and committed notes updates. Supply the existing cache-aside read with its cache write missing expiry, and the update path missing cache deletion.\', \'observable_success\': \'Assertions distinguish the old notes from the new notes, confirm positive TTL after population, confirm -2 immediately after invalidation, and confirm the next cache-aside read returns the changed notes with expiry restored. The learner correctly interprets -1 as an existing key without expiry.\'}}\n\ntolerance_seconds=30\n\nasync def check_freshness(new_notes):\n    async def run():\n        cache=Cache();primary={\'notes\':freshness_contract[\'old_notes\']};key=freshness_contract[\'cache_key\']\n        async def load(): return primary[\'notes\']\n        async def update(notes): primary[\'notes\']=notes\n        await cache.set(key,primary[\'notes\'])\n        no_expiry=await cache.ttl(key);await cache.delete(key)\n        old=await read_notes(cache,key,load,tolerance_seconds)\n        initial_ttl=await cache.ttl(key)\n        assert 0<initial_ttl<=tolerance_seconds<=freshness_contract[\'tolerance_seconds\']\n        await change_notes(cache,key,update,new_notes)\n        absent=await cache.ttl(key)\n        assert absent==-2\n        changed=await read_notes(cache,key,load,tolerance_seconds)\n        final_ttl=await cache.ttl(key)\n        assert 0<final_ttl<=tolerance_seconds\n        return {\'no_expiry\':no_expiry,\'absent_after_delete\':absent,\'old\':old,\'changed\':changed,\'initial_expiry_valid\':0<initial_ttl<=tolerance_seconds,\'final_expiry_valid\':0<final_ttl<=tolerance_seconds,\'primary\':primary[\'notes\']}\n    return await run()\n\nimport inspect\n\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI\n\nif \'app\' not in globals(): app=FastAPI()\n\n@app.post(\'/__exercise_check\')\nasync def exercise_check(payload: dict):\n    result=globals()[payload[\'function\']](*payload.get(\'args\',[]))\n    if inspect.isawaitable(result): result=await result\n    return result\n'

async def read_notes(cache, key, load_notes, seconds):
    cached=await cache.get(key)
    if cached is not None: return cached
    notes=await load_notes()
    await cache.set(key, notes)  # TODO: finite expiry within the supplied tolerance
    return notes

async def change_notes(cache, key, committed_update, new_notes):
    await committed_update(new_notes)

_SUPPLIED_SCAFFOLD = 'import asyncio,csv,time,json\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nclass PrimaryStore:\n    def __init__(self):\n        with open(Path("orders.csv"), newline="") as handle:\n            self.rows = {int(r["id"]): r for r in csv.DictReader(handle)}\n        self.calls = []\n    async def load_order(self, order_id, representation):\n        self.calls.append((order_id, representation))\n        r = self.rows[order_id]\n        if representation == "notes":\n            return {"order_id":order_id, "notes":r["note"]}\n        return {"order_id":order_id, "quantity":int(r["quantity"])}\n    async def committed_notes_update(self, order_id, notes):\n        self.rows[order_id]["note"] = notes\n\nclass Cache:\n    # Endpoint-ready adapter for cache-aside; not raw Redis serialization.\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.entries = {}; self.expiry = {}; self.calls = []\n    async def get(self, key):\n        self.calls.append(("get", key))\n        if key in self.expiry and self.expiry[key] <= time.monotonic():\n            self.entries.pop(key, None); self.expiry.pop(key, None)\n        return self.entries.get(key)\n    async def set(self, key, value, ex=None):\n        self.calls.append(("set", key, ex)); self.entries[key] = value\n        if ex is None: self.expiry.pop(key, None)\n        else: self.expiry[key] = time.monotonic()+ex\n    async def delete(self, key):\n        self.calls.append(("delete", key)); self.entries.pop(key, None); self.expiry.pop(key, None)\n    async def ttl(self, key):\n        if await self.get(key) is None: return -2\n        if key not in self.expiry: return -1\n        return max(0, int(self.expiry[key]-time.monotonic()))\n\nclass FailingCache(Cache):\n    def __init__(self, operation, exception):\n        super().__init__(); self.operation=operation; self.exception=exception\n    async def get(self, key):\n        if self.operation == "get": raise self.exception("synthetic cache lookup failure")\n        return None\n    async def set(self, key, value, ex=None):\n        if self.operation == "set": raise self.exception("synthetic cache population failure")\n        return await super().set(key,value,ex)\n\nfreshness_contract={\'order_id\': 42, \'cache_key\': \'order:42:notes\', \'old_notes\': \'old note 42\', \'new_notes\': \'new note 42\', \'tolerance_seconds\': 30, \'ttl_sentinels\': {\'no_expiry\': -1, \'absent_key\': -2}, \'source_concept_id\': \'cache.freshness_policy\', \'objective\': "Implement expiry and write invalidation for one cached order. Choose a positive TTL within a stated stale-data tolerance, assign it when caching the order\'s notes, delete the cache key after a committed notes change, and verify the next cache-aside read returns the changed notes.", \'success\': \'The cached notes have a positive remaining TTL no greater than the chosen lifetime. After the primary notes change and the cache key is deleted, ttl(key) returns -2 before the next read. That read returns the new notes and repopulates the cache with expiry.\', \'live_code\': {\'required_skill\': \'Use Redis string-entry expiry, key deletion after a successful primary write, and TTL inspection within an existing async cache-aside implementation.\', \'allowed_syntax\': [\'async def\', \'await\', \'variable assignment\', \'if\', \'return\', \'assert\', \'string literals\', \'integer literals\', \'comparisons\', \'await redis.get(key)\', \'await redis.set(key, value, ex=seconds)\', \'await redis.delete(key)\', \'await redis.ttl(key)\', \'calls to supplied order retrieval and committed notes-update helpers\'], \'blocked_syntax\': [\'Redis exception handlers that substitute a primary-store read\', \'retry loops around Redis operations\', \'Redis pipeline or transaction commands\', \'Redis Lua scripting\'], \'starter_state_intent\': \'Provide a connected async Redis client, one order cache key, synthetic old and new notes, a stated stale-data tolerance, and supplied helpers for primary-order retrieval and committed notes updates. Supply the existing cache-aside read with its cache write missing expiry, and the update path missing cache deletion.\', \'observable_success\': \'Assertions distinguish the old notes from the new notes, confirm positive TTL after population, confirm -2 immediately after invalidation, and confirm the next cache-aside read returns the changed notes with expiry restored. The learner correctly interprets -1 as an existing key without expiry.\'}}\n\ntolerance_seconds=30\n\nasync def check_freshness(new_notes):\n    async def run():\n        cache=Cache();primary={\'notes\':freshness_contract[\'old_notes\']};key=freshness_contract[\'cache_key\']\n        async def load(): return primary[\'notes\']\n        async def update(notes): primary[\'notes\']=notes\n        await cache.set(key,primary[\'notes\'])\n        no_expiry=await cache.ttl(key);await cache.delete(key)\n        old=await read_notes(cache,key,load,tolerance_seconds)\n        initial_ttl=await cache.ttl(key)\n        assert 0<initial_ttl<=tolerance_seconds<=freshness_contract[\'tolerance_seconds\']\n        await change_notes(cache,key,update,new_notes)\n        absent=await cache.ttl(key)\n        assert absent==-2\n        changed=await read_notes(cache,key,load,tolerance_seconds)\n        final_ttl=await cache.ttl(key)\n        assert 0<final_ttl<=tolerance_seconds\n        return {\'no_expiry\':no_expiry,\'absent_after_delete\':absent,\'old\':old,\'changed\':changed,\'initial_expiry_valid\':0<initial_ttl<=tolerance_seconds,\'final_expiry_valid\':0<final_ttl<=tolerance_seconds,\'primary\':primary[\'notes\']}\n    return await run()\n\nimport inspect\n\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI\n\nif \'app\' not in globals(): app=FastAPI()\n\n@app.post(\'/__exercise_check\')\nasync def exercise_check(payload: dict):\n    result=globals()[payload[\'function\']](*payload.get(\'args\',[]))\n    if inspect.isawaitable(result): result=await result\n    return result\n'

async def read_notes(cache, key, load_notes, seconds):
    cached=await cache.get(key)
    if cached is not None: return cached
    notes=await load_notes()
    await cache.set(key, notes, ex=seconds)  # TODO: finite expiry within the supplied tolerance
    return notes

async def change_notes(cache, key, committed_update, new_notes):
    await committed_update(new_notes)
    await cache.delete(key)
# END LEARNER EDITS

### s06 · explainer · Choose the boundary: Safe failure policy
(mechanism) Catch Redis connection and timeout exceptions at the cache boundary, then apply the declared outage policy rather than letting the incidental exception decide. ⟦B.f1 B:tb1 B:tb2 B:tb3⟧
(mechanism) With an acceleration-only cache and available primary store, load and return primary data on the miss path even if cache reading or writing fails. ⟦B.f2 B:tb1 B:tb2 B:tb3⟧
(mechanism) Set finite socket_connect_timeout and socket_timeout on the Redis client to bound connection and response waits. ⟦B.f3 B:tb1 B:tb2 B:tb3⟧
(mechanism) Use a controlled failing cache dependency to verify the primary result or declared failure outcome. Cache failure alone does not establish lost primary data. ⟦B.f4 B:tb1 B:tb2 B:tb3⟧
[CODE python]
from redis.asyncio import Redis
from redis.exceptions import ConnectionError, TimeoutError

# finite_connect_seconds and finite_response_seconds are finite service settings.
redis = Redis(socket_connect_timeout=finite_connect_seconds,
              socket_timeout=finite_response_seconds)

async def safe_read(cache, key, load, seconds):
    try:
        cached = await cache.get(key)
    except (ConnectionError, TimeoutError):
        cached = None
    if cached is not None:
        return cached
    value = await load()
    try:
        await cache.set(key, value, ex=seconds)
    except (ConnectionError, TimeoutError):
        pass
    return value

# Supplied controlled substitute and registered primary result:
for operation in contract["failure_operations"]:
    for exception in (ConnectionError, TimeoutError):
        cache = FailingCache(operation, exception)
        assert await safe_read(cache, key, load, seconds) == contract["primary_value"]
  annotations: ['Lookup exception boundary.', 'Primary loading stays outside cache catches; population failure does not discard the available value.', 'Controlled lookup and population failures verify the acceleration-only policy.']

### s07 · explainer · Trace the distinction: Safe failure policy
(mental_model) Test both lookup and population failure with controlled Redis connection and timeout exceptions. The fixture’s cache is acceleration only: available primary data still returns. Keep exception handling around cache operations, not the primary loader. Finite waits bound the cache failure; they do not promise every primary store is available. ⟦B.f1 B.f2 B.f3 B.f4 B:tb4 B:tb5⟧

### s08 · check · Distinguish: Safe failure policy
Check your understanding — 
QUESTION [choice, stretch, grading=client] The cache is acceleration-only and primary data is available. A Redis population write raises ConnectionError or TimeoutError after a miss. What outcome follows the declared policy?
  ✔ o1: Return the available primary-store value while handling the cache connection/timeout failure at the cache boundary.
  ✘ o2: Discard the available primary value because the cache write failed.
      why: An incidental acceleration-layer failure should not discard the authoritative value under this policy.
  ✘ o3: Mask all primary-store errors inside the same Redis exception handler.
      why: Exception handling belongs at the cache operations, not around unrelated primary failures.
  feedback✔: You preserved the intended boundary. Catch Redis connection and timeout exceptions at the cache boundary so the application can apply its declared cache-outage policy rather than letting an incidental cache exception choose the outcome. When the cache is only an acceleration layer and the primary store is available, the miss path can load and return the primary-store value without a successful cache read or write. Verify the policy with a controlled failing cache dependency, asserting the primary-store result or the declared failure outcome rather than assuming that a cache outage is equivalent to lost primary data.  feedback✘: Review the relevant boundary and compare each required outcome. configured_cache = Redis(socket_connect_timeout=1, socket_timeout=1)

async def safe_read(cache, key, load_primary, seconds):
    try:
        value = await cache.get(key)
    except (ConnectionError, TimeoutError):
        value = None
    if value is not None:
        return value
    value = await load_primary()
    try:
        await cache.set(key, value, ex=seconds)
    except (ConnectionError, TimeoutError):
        pass
    return value

### s10 · recap · Keep the boundaries clear
(takeaway) Contrast a key without expiry with an absent key. Invalidation removes the stale copy so the next read reloads. ⟦A.f1 A.f2 A.f3 A.f4 A:tb2 A:tb3 A:tb4 A:tb5⟧

## REMEDIATION STEPS (shown only after a wrong answer)
### r01 · Try again: interpret ttl and verify freshness after invalidation
(mechanism) ttl(key) reports remaining seconds, -1 for no expiry, and -2 for absence. Change the primary record and verify invalidation makes the next read return the changed value. ⟦A.f4 A:tb3 A:tb4 A:tb5⟧
(misconception) Contrast a key without expiry with an absent key. Invalidation removes the stale copy so the next read reloads. ⟦A.f1 A.f2 A.f3 A.f4 A:tb2 A:tb3 A:tb4 A:tb5⟧
### r02 · Try again: select and configure expiry for staleness tolerance
(mechanism) Choose TTL from tolerated staleness. Shorter TTL refreshes sooner but increases primary reads; longer TTL reduces reads but may serve older values longer. ⟦A.f1 A:tb2 A:tb4⟧
(mechanism) For a Redis string entry, set(key, value, ex=seconds) assigns expiry when writing. ⟦A.f2 A:tb3 A:tb4⟧
(mechanism) After the primary record changes, delete its cache key. The next cache-aside read then reloads instead of returning the old copy. ⟦A.f3 A:tb3 A:tb4 A:tb5⟧
(mechanism) ttl(key) reports remaining seconds, -1 for no expiry, and -2 for absence. Change the primary record and verify invalidation makes the next read return the changed value. ⟦A.f4 A:tb3 A:tb4 A:tb5⟧
(misconception) Contrast a key without expiry with an absent key. Invalidation removes the stale copy so the next read reloads. ⟦A.f1 A.f2 A.f3 A.f4 A:tb2 A:tb3 A:tb4 A:tb5⟧
### r03 · Try again: determine whether cache failure changes authoritative outcomes
(mechanism) Catch Redis connection and timeout exceptions at the cache boundary, then apply the declared outage policy rather than letting the incidental exception decide. ⟦B.f1 B:tb2 B:tb4 B:tb5⟧
(mechanism) With an acceleration-only cache and available primary store, load and return primary data on the miss path even if cache reading or writing fails. ⟦B.f2 B:tb3 B:tb4 B:tb5⟧
(mechanism) Use a controlled failing cache dependency to verify the primary result or declared failure outcome. Cache failure alone does not establish lost primary data. ⟦B.f4 B:tb3 B:tb4⟧
(misconception) Contrast unavailable acceleration with unavailable primary data. Apply the declared policy without masking a primary failure. ⟦B.f1 B.f2 B.f3 B.f4 B:tb2 B:tb3 B:tb4 B:tb5⟧
### r04 · Try again: identify finite cache connection and response waits
(mechanism) Set finite socket_connect_timeout and socket_timeout on the Redis client to bound connection and response waits. ⟦B.f3 B:tb3 B:tb4 B:tb5⟧
(misconception) Contrast unavailable acceleration with unavailable primary data. Apply the declared policy without masking a primary failure. ⟦B.f1 B.f2 B.f3 B.f4 B:tb2 B:tb3 B:tb4 B:tb5⟧
